This is of historical importance because previously difficult work has been assessed differently from in-house services. - That's an explanation. If there is an exit of women for a baby break during the career and the return to work is not so good – this also explains a few percent pay gap. The fact in Austria is that four out of ten women work part-time. It is therefore true that even hourly wages at full-time and part-time for the same work, when performed by women, are up to 22 percent lower at part-time. In addition, the more labour market participation there is in a country, the greater the number of pay shears. In Malta or Italy, for example, where the employment rate of women is lower, the pay gap is also lower. [[President Mag. Prammer gives the bell sign.]] I will report later on on what measures should be taken in this context.

On the other hand, more and more condemned criminals, who are to get a negative asylum notice and be postponed, would often be already at the airport under the most adventurous reasons’ – often supported, I add, by NGOs – “to make a new asylum application that needs to be treated again and binds our hands”. – quoting. That's exactly the situation. That's where the laws changed. It can't be that someone comes to us, lives here for perhaps half a year or a year, gets criminal, is condemned, and, after being sentenced, makes an asylum application – and we Austrians are being papered. Such an asylum application should not even be accepted. Nor can it be that we continue to maintain new applications by law. If someone makes an asylum application and has been legally rejected, it is practical today that a new application be made. If this is rejected, a third – newer – application will be filed. And if this is rejected, a fourth – newer – application will be made. So you're papering, please, the rule of law of Austria – and you're responsible for it because you haven't removed this bid for innovation. That's exactly where it's going! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Furthermore, Mr. Willfried Kavarnik says: “More and more of these people are taking advantage of the legal opportunity to make almost unlimited applications for asylum and thus withdraw from deportation for years.” And that's what you want to reward as a federal government in the end? You want to reward that in the end?! - No! [[Abg. Oillinger: We can bring you something...]] There we say clearly and clearly no: Instead of a right of residence, the right of asylum must be strengthened on the one hand, and finally, in the case of abusers, the consistent deportation must be carried out. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We want a procedural reduction. The procedure shall not exceed three months. There must also be a strict ban on innovation, and a final line must finally be drawn under the Schindluder in this area. [[Abg. Oils: Bullshits!]] We demand the inclusion of fingerprints in the residence permit card and tough measures against criminal asylum seekers. Furthermore, the procedural setting-up of proceedings when the applicant's attempt to conceal the asylum status by the asylum seekers, the imposition of proceedings in the case of fraud by the authorities concerning the applicant's identity, the setting-up of proceedings in the event of a self-indebted non-documentation of travel or identity documents and the immediate designation of all procedural settings. [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Korun.]] With this right to stay, you do exactly the opposite. They legalize asylum fraud, you are almost still providing state aid towards asylum fraud. That's basically scandalous! I say quite frankly that the Austrian population has been heavily burdened by your policy in this area over the last few years. She didn't deserve such a thing. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] It is also quite right that you are not prepared to recognize the concerns and needs of the Austrians and finally to distinguish between really persecuted people to whom we have protection, of course on time, and those asylum abusers and frauds to be postponed. ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e11687	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Values Watchers on the galleries and in front of the TV screens! I would like to return to the point of departure, because it seems that some have not yet come to terms with the fact that this is not a new law, but a repair order from the Constitutional Court, which has withdrawn the humanitarian stay already recorded in the law insofar as he has said it is only by its own initiative, one cannot make a request. There was no excitement on your part at that time! But now, today and here, you're excited about this, and that seems a little strange to me. [[Abg. Strache: Because you do something that the Constitutional Court does not like...!]] I'm going back to the draft assessment. The first draft assessment, which was presented by the Minister of the Interior in December of the previous year, took place on 22 December. At the national conference of the Heads of State or Government there were some criticisms, especially with regard to the old cases, the sponsorship and the advisory board; especially the advisory board had no uniform federal rules in the bill at that time. There is a revised government template, and many aspects have been incorporated into this revised government template. For example, if the designation in accordance with Article 8 of the ECHR is inadmissible in the asylum or foreign law proceedings, it is now possible here for the first time – and I stress once again: for the first time! – to grant a residence permit; and both in the Asylum Act, in the Foreign Police Act and in the Settlement and Residence Act, this is regulated in accordance with the case law of the Constitutional Court with a criteria catalogue. This catalogue of criteria has also been criticised in its original form, but it has been amended on the basis of that. A case-by-case examination shall be carried out in each of these cases. And if, in such a case, an authorisation is granted on its own initiative or on request, there are two different variants: those of the unlimited and the limited authorisation of establishment. This is a good sign because it proves that the humanitarian aspects are already integrated into the regular procedure. For the old cases, these are those persons who have been since the 1st century. In the Federal Republic of Germany, there is now also the possibility of obtaining a branch permit in May 2004. In doing so, the authority has to review – and these are exactly the points you are always calling for – the degree of integration, self-sustainability, school and vocational training and the knowledge of the German language. And if in the expert hearing Professor Funk has meant that he is in a fundamental relationship of tension in this assessment, I think, the Federal Minister will have an eye on her that she is training her authorities so that she prepares them for the execution in an appropriate way. Self-sustainability with the sponsorship – one of the points that were so strongly criticized – has also been changed. There is now no longer just the sponsorship in front of the notary, there is also it in front of a domestic court. It was reduced from five years to three years. The third point was the obligations of health insurance, accommodation and maintenance. With regard to the absence of the mandatory sponsorship: I have not heard in this debate today that it is only taking place by means of a notarial act and that it corresponds almost to the statement of liability in the field of visas already in force and is to be applied only more subsidiaryly. The advisory board, which is available to the woman minister in the decisions, has been set up differently, and it is without any doubt for the municipal or municipal representative to contact mayors who live on the ground. I am convinced that this repair – it is not a new draft for a new law – has taken place, the mandate of the Constitutional Court, in relation to the application, in the sense of a good middle of humanity and the rule of law. Additional suggestions for improvement could also be put in place, and it's not the way you're trying to communicate it here: It was not an order to create a new foreign right here and now, it was a repair. And if we want to create a new foreign right, I invite you to do so because it is up to us to develop it further.

Point Report by the Committee on Health, Public Health and Consumer Protection on amendment 159/A by Mr Dr. Sabine Oberhauser, Dr. Erwin Rasinger, colleagues concerning a federal law amending the Health Telecommunications Act (138 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e15887	We're on the 7th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Spindelberger spoke. I wish you every time: 3 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e15915	Mr President. High house! If we are dealing with today's request for the amendment of the Health Telecommunications Act, it is simply a question of extending the transitional arrangements already adopted by another year, specifically until 31.12.2009, which means that the existing administrative penalties will be postponed again. But why this step becomes necessary is also obvious. The Health Telecommunications Act is a very, very delicate issue, namely the use and application of new technologies and data processing throughout the health sector. This effort to make use of these technologies is also based on a clear understanding, namely the recognition, these quantities of health data and these quantities of medical knowledge, which results from rapid medical progress alone, to make the best possible use for all of us. This would, logically, require adequate information and communication support. As well as it may sound, it also means in practice that we are faced with enormous problems, because the processing and transmission of these health data in various data networks results in a huge risk potential in terms of possible violations of the confidentiality and integrity of our health data. It is therefore necessary to introduce Austrian-wide harmonisation of current different approaches to data security measures or to develop the standard for data security measures. As I mentioned earlier, it was planned that a definitive regulation should already be adopted by the end of 2008, which is why Federal Minister Kdolsky has also sent a regulation on this subject to review. However, given that the views on this Regulation have been negative by the Bank, the Data Protection Council has, as such an important matter, namely the protection of sensitive data, proposed at its meeting in December, extended these transitional periods until 31. On December 1, 2009, the Commission agreed to agree to the agreement and to bring it into play today. This has also been pointed out by the members of the Data Protection Council that there will also be an ELGA law, i.e. the Electronic Health Act, by the end of this year. Thus, we all have sufficient time to prepare all the necessary changes or adaptations to the Health Telecommunications Act. I am pleased that all this proposal will be accepted.

It is 90 to 180 days in the Transparency Directive. In Austria, this period has been between 24 and 36 months. In 2001, Austria had already been sentenced by the European Court of Justice for the first time to understand this scheme. ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e16910	The next item is the speech by Mr Stöger. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e16931	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. I am pleased that I have a brief opportunity to inform you about the changes to the ASVG and the accompanying laws. Austria was sentenced by the ECJ in July 2008 in a infringement procedure for breach of the Transparency Directive. The reason for this was that the Austrian legal situation did not meet the requirements of the Transparency Directive, insofar as the time limits for inclusion in the so-called refund code were far too long, exactly 24 or 36 months. We were thus far from complying with the Transparency Directive. This Directive lays down a time limit of 90 and 180 days for the inclusion in the yellow or green area of the refund code. The content of the present draft law is therefore the adaptation to the EU rules. It is intended to ensure that the application of a authorised company for the adoption of a proprietary medicinal product in the yellow or green area of the refund code is decided within 90 days, i.e. three months. If the price is also decided, this period may be up to 180 days. Ladies and gentlemen, this implementation is urgently needed to penalise our Republic as well as claims for damages to avoid the pharmaceutical industry for non-implementation of the Directive. Thank you.

According to current law, the occupational pension falls one year after the entry into force of the insurance case. Thus, the consumer – just as he did in the Cattle Prohibition Act – can decide which products he takes, and so also choose animal welfare.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e21476	President. — The debate is closed. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e21498	Mr President. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! After the subject has already been discussed here in the House, I would like to briefly explain my reasons why I will introduce strict limits on trans fats in food. Artificial trans fats are dangerous fats. They do not have a necessary function in the food, but promote cardiovascular diseases. In addition, some cancers, diabetes and other frequent sufferings are discussed. I have decided not to pursue an observation and announcement policy here, but to act. I have therefore sent a transfatty acid regulation in review. The assessment period runs until the end of March. The Regulation limits the use of artificial trans fatty acids in foodstuffs in the sense of preventive health protection. The draft provides for an in-transport bridge of fats and oils in Austria to be banned with more than 2 percent artificial trans fatty acids. For compound foods with a fat content of less than 20 percent, a trans fatty acid content of up to 4 percent should be allowed. A general reduction in total fat intake is a health policy objective promoted by this measure. We follow – and this has already been mentioned – the example of Denmark, where a similar regime has been in force for some time. Danish experience shows broad acceptance of these measures. In Denmark, there has been neither an increase in the price of food nor a reduction in the price of supply, as originally led by the opponents of this measure immediately to the meeting. A scientific publication on the situation in Denmark has shown that de facto it is no longer possible to consume a health-threatening amount of artificial trans fatty acids. This should also be the case in Austria in the future. In Europe, we are the second country to take this step after Denmark. Following the current planning, the Regulation should be able to enter into force in June. I am Convinced that it is a proper measure in the interests of the population and an important objective in terms of health care. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e21880	Next, Mrs Mag. Grossmann speaking. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e21902	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Streumunition is a particularly humane cruel weapon. It not only kills indiscriminately during the fights, it also creates an immeasurable danger to the civilian population for decades after the end of armed conflicts, because the ammunition is distributed throughout the country and the rate of blindness is extremely high. This means that the affected population is still in uncertainty for decades and cannot really move freely and safely in its own country. We all know the images of the cruel injuries and therefore know what these weapons are doing and how they can do to people. In order to end this suffering, Austria, together with a core group of states, has already very early advocated a ban on these weapons under international law. In fact, it was only seven states with Austria that In the end, pioneers were able to move a group of states from one hundred to another to a coalition against clusterunition. The so-called Oslo process has been launched. ==References== Since the Convention on Antipersonnel Mines was signed in Oslo in December 2008, it is likely that the most important disarmament treaty has been a major development of international humanitarian law. The core provisions of the Convention provide for a comprehensive ban on the use, development, production, acquisition, storage, retention and transfer of stock-unitions to provide comprehensive support in terms of medical, psychological care, rehabilitation and social and professional reintegration. It is also to be emphasized – as Mr Plassnik did – that the wide-ranging definition of brimunition, which in fact only excludes high-tech precision weapons. So today it is up to us to ratify this important agreement. I am looking forward to your agreement and hope that many states will follow our example and implement this agreement as quickly as possible.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e22710	Next speaker: Mrs Mag. Muttons. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e22733	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The cultural agreement with the Czech Republic is very welcome, because intensive cooperation and a discussion in the field of education and culture with our neighbouring country has always been a very important and important concern for us. The ratification of the UNESCO Convention to preserve the intangible cultural heritage by the Austrian Parliament is also very positive. The Convention concludes a gap between the 1972 World Heritage Convention and the Convention on the Protection and Promotion of the Variety of Cultural Expressions adopted in 2006. This is what we have all decided together in Parliament. The aim of the UNESCO Convention to be ratified today is to preserve the intangible cultural heritage and at the same time to raise awareness in the population for its importance. Another objective is the creation of a cultural framework that allows the transfer of living cultural forms. This is necessary because in a number of countries, in the face of progressive globalisation, the concern about the threat of losing their cultural heritage is growing. But what can one understand by this very broad term “immaterial forms of culture ”? – These include performances, forms of expression, knowledge and skills, i.e. languages of ethnic minorities or the creation of objects, artifacts and cultural spaces, all that communities see as part of their cultural heritage. In the word culture, many of us immediately think of large theatre or orchestra, but forget about the art of instrument building, a form of immaterial cultural heritage. These forms of culture are not only an expression of cultural diversity, they also promote processes of understanding between people, because the transfer of cultural heritage to the next generation includes educational processes, they set in motion, and access to specific spaces of knowledge will be enabled. The protection of the intangible cultural heritage thus goes far beyond the preservation of folklore. That's, I think, very important. Through this transfer – and I consider it particularly important – the connection to contemporary forms of expression and creativity also takes place. - Thank you.

I've had to think about it myself: What does the farmer mean when he told me: “D’Strassen’s Ich kahre!”? [[Abg. Großruck: The road is slippery!]] ‘Hall’ was smooth. Or when I spoke the other day to the old farmer, with whom I am well-friended, she said, “Mer gave it to nit!” I thought: What did she mean by “mer ”? [[Abg. Grossruck: Professor, you're overshadowing the stenographs!]] Yeah, that's right. I'm going to try and make that phonetically. This “mer ” simply means“ earlier. ” So: There would have been no such thing before. And, and, and so. Does anyone know what “favor ” means? Josef Cap? [[Hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, ……]] He has to fit as I did when I came to Vienna and didn't know many expressions. And now I have to admit that I have to concentrate very much in the East Styrian-Southburg border area in order to understand in detail what the friendly farmer says to me. I think this is a very important intangible cultural heritage! This UNESCO Convention alone will not help us to get it in Austria! You would like to draw your attention to the following: We have spent a lot of money on preserving the beautiful architecture of the Habsburgs – rightly, I am delighted every time I can admire a Baroque castle, that is hard, gripping architecture. The language also does not receive itself. And this kind of dialect diversity, which we thank God in Austria, disappears. It automatically disappears with the increasing mobility of people, with tourism – especially for Tyrol – with general economic development. This is, as it were, a collateral damage to economic development. I think we should spend a little money and make the effort to preserve this intangible cultural heritage, which is an acoustic cultural heritage. This is not only about the etymology of words, but also about the debate and the idiomatic, which is irrevocably lost. For example: Sometimes, when I sit in Parliament late in the evening and I'm upset about something – you're certainly the same – a Kaunertal saying falls in my mind, which is a good one: “Do wasch manchmol hihewiati!” – literally translated “brainful”, means as much as “narrical”. [[Cheerfulness of the speaker.]] I think that has its very own sound color, a very own idiomatic, corresponds to the tribal relationships there in the Kaunertal, with the species of mouth different from valley to valley. I'm just telling the history of Tyrol because I know them well. I still know how I traveled from Kaunertal to the back Ötztal as a half grower – this is the northernmost valley, east – and people didn't understand! These too were Tyroleans, even those were mountain dwellers, but I just didn't understand them. You have to practice for a year, live there, understand, then, like with any other foreign language. These are foreign languages here in the country, but our own! I think we should do something for this important intangible cultural heritage! Thank you.

This means that we will no longer have to do with a repatriation as we have now done. The activity report you have referred to several times: I would like to confirm exactly what Mrs Lapp has said, namely that this activity report has been completely modernised – in terms of more clarity, more effectiveness and, of course, in view of the fact that the Court of Auditors' recommendations will be presented more transparently in the future and thus implemented with greater efficiency. In the statement under the heading ‘Main issues of public financial control’, we have indicated that the Court of Auditors takes its constitutional mandate as a federal federal/country body very seriously and conducts cross-sectional audits in this function. In this activity report, you also have the country comparison with childcare facilities. An important factor to take control seriously – and this is what we are committed to as the body of the National Council – is that the control itself is also subject to an evaluation. For this reason, this year we will have a peer review under the leadership of the German Federal Court of Auditors, which will also involve the Federal Financial Control and the Danish Court of Auditors in order to carry out a location control and to look at where the Austrian Court of Auditors is in international comparison, where there is potential for improvement. This also shows that control is nothing negative, but – if both parts work together – it brings positive success to everyone. In this context, I would also like to point out that, in order to give you an overview of the state of implementation of the Court of Auditors recommendations, we have for the first time carried out a demand procedure on the recommendations published in 2007. To this end, the audited bodies asked what measures were being implemented, what recommendations are in implementation or what recommendations are open. I would like to point out that the picture is not as bad as Mr Grosz has outlined. 75 percent of the recommendations have been implemented or are in implementation. But it is also true that 25 percent of the recommendations are open and it would be important to implement them precisely because in this area there is a high potential for savings, optimization and benefits. I can therefore only appeal that the Court of Auditors' recommendations will also be implemented in the future in terms of administrative reform. Because the Court of Auditors was repeatedly asked what it was doing: He has a difficult function. We fulfil our constitutional function. That is why we are also conducting a demand process. We will also, in order to increase effectiveness, make the outcome of the demand process the basis of follow-up tests to show you how the realization looks. But we are an organ of the National Council, and when it comes to living control and implementing control, I can only point out: You, ladies and gentlemen, are the guardians of control! We have a derived control right, and I believe that together we are able to exercise control in such a way that the use of funds is actually made in an optimized and optimal manner. I would therefore also like to ask you to take advantage of the parliamentary possibilities of interpellation in order to further enhance the efficiency of the implementation of the recommendations. As far as the 13 reports on the agenda are concerned, which were partially discussed in the course of the debate, I can also point out here that in many areas of the will for implementation is very high, but that in some areas of the will for implementation is not given. A number of areas have been discussed here, such as the ÖBB Retirement Report, where a recommendation does not involve a deterioration. No, it is about the fact that, if there is an average additional fee rate, which does not reflect the actual additional charges incurred, and is caused by massive additional costs, one depends on the actual value and not on the flat-rate value. This could result in massive savings for the ÖBB and, of course, for the taxpayer. Finally, I come to the point “Control Fields”, which was also mentioned. I can only say, as far as municipal audits are concerned, that a audit of the Court of Auditors has three effects: We have the effect of controlling the city's citizens so that he then knows that the canal, garbage and other charges are fees and not taxes. This means that the cost recovery principle is respected. And I believe that the citizen is interested and also has a right to know whether the fees are fees, i.e. whether the cost coverage principle is respected. We have the effect for the municipal council, which will then be able to examine certain areas if it can build on a control by an independent control body. We don't have that right now. And we have – also mentioned – an advantage for the community itself. The municipality has enormous tasks to fulfil in the context of general care, and here the question arises whether the transfer revenue corresponds to the transfer expenditure. And who would be a better guardian or more objective finder than the Court of Auditors? We then have the opportunity to represent exactly the balance or imbalance between transfer revenue and transfer expenditure. On the point raised by Mr Gaßner, on protection against natural hazards: This is not a question of criticizing us, but we have shown that municipal compensation must be forced among the communities. For the community which applied for a protective measure would have In many cases the cost should also be borne on its own, although other municipalities would also benefit from this cost reduction. On the other hand, the other communities – often the beneficiaries – would not be prepared, in part, to bear these costs so that protection measures can actually be taken. This lack of cooperation in the final effect means that necessary and already recognised protective measures are not taken. Finally, I would like to thank you once again for the excellent cooperation, I am already looking forward to further cooperation with you and I am also looking for taxpayers to go in the direction that we share our opportunities for the recommendations to be implemented and, in the future, for optimization potentials, savings potentials and efficiency gains to be used. Thank you for your cooperation and attention. Thank you.

I would like to point out two things which I have found in this proposal and which I consider to be problematic. One is the Liberation-Self-Law of 1946. I would like to inform you briefly of what that means, namely: “Weighs to criminal acts... which between the day of liberation” – that is, different according to the federal state, 1945, the division – “... and the 25. In order to ensure the establishment of the Republic of Austria as a democratic state, to employ national socialist assets of public interest or to provide moral or material satisfaction to victims of national socialist rule, it is not necessary to initiate criminal proceedings, provided that the threat of punishment does not exceed ten years’. That is, for six months robbery, rape, theft, body injury, necessity, if they took place in connection with the restoration of the Republic, were free of punishment. It is certainly an understandable law from the point of view of the time, but if I think of today’s legal order and legal situation and of the attitude towards such things, I would have expected the Greens to never forget that way, according to the motto “Never forget!”, but also in the opinion: We are now considering this with the view of today's time!, rather for the repeal of such a law. That is not the case, however. The second point, which is very problematic in this Green Bill, concerns the so-called mixing procedures, mixed convictions. There have been convictions, which have been made on the one hand by political laws and, on the other, by the normal criminal acts, by normal criminal laws, on the other. A typical example of this is the desertion and in connection with it a murder. These mixed convictions were subsequently handled in such a way that the political judgment had been repealed and a new procedure was initiated on the criminal judgment. It is correct to note that 64 years after the war – this is just now, as this request has been made here – all the crimes have been delayed, not But murder. It is then explicitly stated that this rule of law would in this case bring a special period of persecution for murder. – I do not see why deserters who have become murderers should be treated differently from other citizens. For this reason alone, we will not agree to the legislative proposal in this form.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e25801	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! This draft law is based on a French model. So we do not use the authorship for us. There are similar language protection laws in many European countries, such as Poland and Latvia. The aim is the same everywhere, namely to strengthen the measures, to strengthen the protection of the respective national language, because the language is not only the most important means of understanding available to us, but it is also the most important source of culture. In recent years, we have had to see a number of misdevelopments. Many young people, for example, who use the new media in particular, have the “Internet” keyword, which is evidenced by linguistic expressiveness. [[Abg. Booker: “Internet” is not German either!]] Part of the advertising industry and also many semi-educated people we know from the “side views” in the ORF want or can no longer express themselves clearly and clearly in their own native language. Further: German as a scientific language has also been on the retreat since the 1920s of the last century. More and more courses, not only in the field of computer science, are held in English, but one of course has to be kept clear in this context: If you really believe that important things must only be said in English in the future, then you will evaluate your own language and also your own research in the field of science. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] The fact that our concerns are not an ideologically based one, but rather a cultural policy proves that politicians have used various world-wide directions, such as the former German Bundestag President Wolfgang Thierse, as well as Johannes Rau of the SPD, MPD representatives, but also representatives of the Liberal Free Democrats in the Federal Republic of Germany. That's what I'm gonna end up with. The great German humanist Victor von Bülow, perhaps better known as Loriot, has found: “The angling of our language is gradually increasing into a monstrosity.” There's nothing to add to that. I believe that this draft law is over-ripe – and I ask for approval.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e25843	The next item is the speech by Mrs Mag. Muttons. Also 2 minutes desired speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-03-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00017_d7e25865	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, we are very positive about the fundamental intensification of this request to protect the German language. It is also defined in our federal constitution as a state language. And also in other laws such as the School Education Act, German is anchored as a language of teaching. We have even agreed to a joint motion for a resolution that German should be further promoted as an EU working language, for it is already official. All in all, then, I do seem to have sufficient protection for the German language. Whether a federal law, as proposed here in the application, constitutes an appropriate instrument for the promotion of German language, is another question. I already have my reservations. But we will certainly be able to discuss it sufficiently in the Committee on Culture. Multilingualism, ladies and gentlemen, is of enormous importance in our global world. I am thinking of tourism, is thinking of international organisations, of exports and the like. Here your request seems to me to be far too inconceivable and restrictive – for example, the command to write inscriptions in German in public space. Does that mean we're going to have to disassemble all foreign-language names at the airport now? A note that is very important to me, ladies and gentlemen. Although German is established as a state language, it is also the duty to promote and safeguard the language and culture of the indigenous peoples. And I find very interesting scientific research in this context, according to which a foreign language or a second language is better learned when you are fit in the mother tongue. That's what we should consider. In the application itself, the explanatory statement refers to the French language protection law, which, as far as I know, means in the translation: Law on the use of the French language. I'm curious about how you'd deal with it. For example, if you want to have everything in German, how do you want to translate “Pommes frites” and “Ketchup”? How do you want to translate the word “Airbag”? Maybe with “airbag”? In addition: Look Once you turn to the words in the German language, then you will make eyes and you will no longer come out of amazement, for language is also a living thing. Thank you.

We are for school partnerships that contribute to integration. Of course, good German knowledge is of great importance. But I do not believe that this can be enforced with an obligation to speak German. This certainly does not solve the real social problems!
